The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

EBITDA.

  • Company adjusted EBITA 105.3 (FY2025)
  • plus D&A excluding PPA 14.1
  • = company adjusted EBITDA 119.4 (28.1%)
  • less recurring roll-up costs of about 6.0
  • = 113.4 (26.7%), which equals FactSet's brokers'-basis FY2025 actual of 114 (26.7%). This is the basis of my EBITDA margin input.

FactSet's history line "EBITDA (reported, FactSet operating)" of 110 (25.9%) is a third basis. Do not read consensus against it.

EBIT. Consensus EBIT is after PPA. FY2026: EBITDA 133, less D&A of about 17, less PPA of about 26, gives about 90, against consensus EBIT of 88.5. My model's EBITDA minus D&A is EBITA, before PPA. To compare with consensus EBIT, deduct PPA of about 26.0, 27.5 and 29.5 for FY2026 to FY2028.

EPS: four bases. Never mix them.

  • FactSet brokers' FY2025 actual of EUR 0.088. It reconciles (my reconciliation) to reported net income of 31.3, plus IAC of 14.7, plus about 9.8 of non-cash contingent-consideration items, with little tax relief: about 46.6m over 529m shares. So most brokers deduct PPA. DNB Carnegie adds back PPA and uses a 25% tax rate (0.140 for FY2026).
  • My model basis (the cross-check row): excludes PPA and one-off IAC; includes recurring roll-up costs and all financial items.
  • After-PPA basis (close to UBS and Nordea): model basis, less PPA after tax (about 22% tax relief), plus non-cash financial items, taxed about 1.5 points higher. This gives 0.091, 0.111, 0.131, 0.155 and 0.179 for FY2026 to FY2030.
  • Reported IFRS: 0.069, 0.099, 0.121, 0.146 and 0.171.

Against the consensus median (0.100, 0.121, 0.152).

  • On levels, my after-PPA basis is -9%, -8.5% and -14%.
  • On growth from FY2026, which is mostly known after nine months, it is about in line for FY2027 and about -6% for FY2028.
  • My central like-for-like judgement is about -4% (FY2027) and -10% (FY2028).

The model-basis EPS in the cross-check is above the consensus median only because of its basis: it excludes PPA amortisation of EUR 26m to 33m a year. It is not a more optimistic forecast.

Free cash flow.

  • FactSet's history is operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex only.
  • FactSet's consensus FCF (23, 58, 78) does not reconcile to consensus operating cash flow less capex (53.5, 80.5, 99). It likely deducts leases or earn-outs. Its definition is unclear.

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

EPS is on the model basis: excluding PPA amortisation and one-off IAC, after recurring roll-up costs, all net financial items, the adjusted tax rate and about EUR 1.5m a year of minorities, over average diluted shares (531m to 539m).

Free cash flow is operating cash flow after cash interest and cash tax, less capex including intangibles and lease payments, before acquisitions and earn-outs. It counts the working capital of acquired businesses as the model does. It adds back non-cash financial items and deducts one-off IAC paid in cash. It is therefore about EUR 5m higher than a mechanical "EBITDA less tax, financial items, capex, working capital and leases" in FY2026 (EUR 10m of non-cash financial items), and EUR 1.5m to 2.5m higher in later years.

On the after-PPA basis comparable with most brokers, EPS is 0.091, 0.111, 0.131, 0.155 and 0.179; reported EPS is 0.069, 0.099, 0.121, 0.146 and 0.171.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Vimian Group AB (VIMIAN-SE), a Stockholm-listed companion-animal health group reporting in euros, with four segments: Specialty Pharma (Nextmune: allergy diagnostics and immunotherapy, dermatology, nutrition), MedTech (Movora veterinary orthopaedics, iM3 and AllAccem veterinary dentistry, Norfolk Medical Products soft-tissue surgery), Veterinary Services (VetFamily buying group for independent clinics, plus co-owned clinics) and Diagnostics (Indical livestock diagnostics, I-Vet and OvaCyte companion-animal diagnostics)

Request: T-D1

Peers: Swedencare (closest Stockholm-listed companion-animal roll-up; leverage and organic misses 2024-2026); Vetoquinol (VETO-FR; delivered a margin plan through mix, range simplification and in-housing); Virbac (VIRP-FR; European specialty animal-health margin progression); CVS Group (CVSG-GB; veterinary consolidator with adjusted-earnings practice and contingent consideration); Dechra Pharmaceuticals (formerly listed, now private; acquisitive veterinary pharma); Lifco, Addtech, AddLife and Indutrade (decentralised Nordic serial acquirers; margin and cash discipline through cycles); IDEXX Laboratories (installed-base diagnostics benchmark for the Diagnostics pivot); Arthrex Vet Systems and the DePuy Synthes veterinary business (private or unit-level orthopaedic implant competitors) Operating conditions: US veterinary clinic visits falling for eighteen consecutive quarters to Q3 2026 (Vetsource and Morgan Stanley, October 2026); the 2026 companion-animal price war (Zoetis gross-to-net rebates and promotions, Elanco's Zenrelia, Merck's Numelvi); corporate clinic consolidation and centralised tendering in the US, UK and Nordics; higher-for-longer interest rates; a weaker US dollar against the euro in 2025-2026; possible US tariffs on imported medical devices; the UK CMA remedies (final decision 24 March 2026, binding order 22 September 2026) Inputs:

  • Margin recovery. Can Vimian raise its group adjusted EBITA margin after it fell from 26.3% in 2023 to 24.8% in 2025 and 24.3% in Q3 2026, while organic growth ran at 9%, 12% and 11% in Q1 to Q3 2026 (company reports)? Which segment levers have precedents?
  • Diagnostics' companion-animal pivot: segment margin 13.8% in Q1 and 7.2% in Q3 2026; management expects improvement "from 2027".
  • MedTech's January 2026 move of US orthopaedics to a field sales force.
  • Dental consumables mix (SANOS sealant, Clarity XR).
  • Veterinary Services' margin "normalising around 28.5%" (management, Q3 2026).
  • Organic growth without a market recovery. Can high single-digit or double-digit organic growth be sustained through cross-selling (about 18% of group sales, Q3 2026 presentation) and professional education (48,000 professionals educated in 2025, annual report)? MedTech's 15% organic growth in Q3 2026 came from a base of -1% in FY2025.
  • Cash conversion. Can it improve after net working capital rose from EUR 96.6m at end-2025 to EUR 116.4m at 30 September 2026, with inventory at EUR 98.3m (Q3 interim report)? Has any direct-to-clinic animal-health supplier converting distributors to direct sales lowered its working capital per euro of revenue?
  • Self-funded acquisitions. Can bolt-ons at about 9x EBITDA (CFO, Norfolk, October 2026) be funded without equity, within a 3.0x leverage ceiling, given the 2022 directed issue and the April 2024 SEK 1.6bn rights issue?
  • The new team's record. What does the record of the new chief executive (appointed November 2025) and the rebuilt segment leadership (MedTech April 2026, Specialty Pharma October 2026) show about delivery? Focus: Has any decentralised animal-health or Nordic serial-acquirer roll-up lifted its group margin and its cash conversion at the same time as stepping up bolt-on acquisitions, and what distinguished those that did? Which of Vimian's planned margin levers rest on strengths it has already demonstrated, and which on strengths it would need to acquire?

Request: T-D2

Peers: Swedencare (adjusted measures, earn-outs, North American volatility); CVS Group (adjusted EPS, contingent consideration, interest-rate swaps); Dechra Pharmaceuticals (formerly listed; adjusted-earnings practice in an acquisitive veterinary pharma); AddLife and Lifco (Nordic acquirers reporting EBITA with large amortisation of acquired intangibles); Vetoquinol and Virbac (tax rates and financial items in European animal health); Zoetis and Elanco (pricing and volume volatility in companion-animal therapeutics); IDEXX Laboratories (diagnostics benchmark) Operating conditions: falling US clinic visits; the 2026 dermatology and parasiticide price war; corporate clinic consolidation; higher-for-longer rates with floating-rate debt; an unhedged US dollar exposure (38% of 2025 revenue); possible US device tariffs; livestock disease-outbreak cycles in diagnostics Inputs:

  • Concentration of drivers. How concentrated are the drivers separating Vimian's next four years from 2021-2025?
  • Segment organic growth: MedTech US orthopaedics against dental; Specialty Pharma allergy and dermatology against US retail nutrition campaign phasing, which swung Q2 2026 to 14% reported and Q3 2026 to 7% against 11% underlying.
  • The Diagnostics margin.
  • Central costs: EUR 3.0m in Q3 2026 against 1.8m a year earlier (Q3 2026 call).
  • Capitalised R&D and software: EUR 7.7m in the first nine months of 2026 against EUR 3.6m (interim report).
  • Net working capital.
  • The acquisition pace.
  • Below-EBITA lines. How volatile are they, and what drives them?
  • Net financial items: EUR 20.6m in 2025 and EUR 18.2m in the first nine months of 2026, including EUR 4.5m of probability adjustments on contingent consideration (interim report).
  • Tax rate: 44% (2023), 33% (2024), 29% (2025), 35% (Q1 2026).
  • Amortisation of acquired intangibles: EUR 23.8m in 2025.
  • Items affecting comparability: EUR 14.7m in 2025.
  • Related-party licensing income: EUR 9.7m in 2025.
  • Dispersion of past outcomes. What was the historical dispersion of one-year EBITA and EPS outcomes against start-of-year consensus for Vimian (2022-2026) and for the named peers? Which single events widened it: the VOI litigation, the 2022 organic slowdown, the 2025 MedTech decline, campaign phasing, currency?
  • The EPS-basis spread. How wide is the FY2027 outcome range once the spread of broker EPS bases is accounted for? FactSet showed FY2026 broker EPS from EUR 0.089 (UBS) to EUR 0.140 (DNB Carnegie) on 10 November 2026.
  • Exposure to one or two drivers. How often were forecasts met when they rested on a margin recovery plus lower financial costs, in acquisitive animal-health and healthcare-services companies? Focus: How wide and how skewed is the range of plausible FY2027 revenue, adjusted EBITA, EPS and free cash flow outcomes for Vimian, and which one or two drivers (segment margin, working capital, contingent-consideration revaluations, campaign phasing) have historically moved outcomes most?

Request: T-I1

Peers: Swedencare (guidance and consensus cuts 2025-2026); CVS Group (consensus behaviour around strategy updates and buybacks); Zoetis (2026 guidance cut and later raise of adjusted EPS guidance, 5 November 2026); Elanco (2027 targets, November 2026); IDEXX Laboratories (clinic-visit outlook); Vetoquinol and Virbac (European animal-health guidance practice); Lifco, Addtech and AddLife (Nordic acquirers with no annual guidance) Operating conditions: a first Capital Markets Day five years after listing (24 November 2026); falling US clinic visits; the 2026 companion-animal price war; higher-for-longer rates; weaker US dollar; an active bolt-on M&A phase (five acquisitions in 2026, including Norfolk Medical Products, closed about 2 November 2026) Inputs:

  • The Capital Markets Day. Vimian gives no annual numerical guidance. Its only target is adjusted EBITA above EUR 300m by 2030 ("over half organic", "without further capital injections", leverage below 3.0x), set in March 2024 and reaffirmed at Q3 2026. What did comparable first Capital Markets Days by Nordic roll-ups and animal-health companies do to long-term targets and to consensus?
  • Management's qualitative signals, set against consensus:
  • Veterinary Services margin around 28.5%.
  • Diagnostics improving from 2027.
  • Capex about 4% of revenue.
  • "Double-digit organic growth" ambition.
  • Norfolk earnings-enhancing from 2027.
  • Consensus (FactSet, 10 November 2026): revenue EUR 484m, 539m and 588m; EBITDA EUR 133m, 153m and 170m; EPS EUR 0.100, 0.121 and 0.152 for FY2026 to FY2028; capex EUR 18m, 15m and 19m.
  • Revision breadth. FY2027 revisions over twelve months: sales +7%, EBITDA +4%, EPS -7% (FactSet). What explains the divergence between EBITDA and EPS revisions, and is it likely to continue?
  • Individual brokers:
  • Jefferies' adjusted EBITA margin assumptions of 24.9% for 2027 (50 basis points below consensus) and 70 basis points below consensus for 2028 (28 October 2026).
  • DNB Carnegie's net debt to EBITDA of 1.3x for 2027e and 0.6x for 2028e (23 October 2026).
  • Does consensus include unannounced acquisitions, and how does each broker treat PPA amortisation, items affecting comparability and non-cash contingent-consideration charges?
  • What each outcome would need. What would raise, hold or cut FY2027-28 consensus at the Q4 2026 (11 February 2027), Q1 2027 and Q2 2027 reports? Focus: Over the next 18 months, are FY2027-28 consensus revenue, EBITDA and EPS more likely to be raised, held or cut, and in which direction will the Capital Markets Day move the 2030 target and the sell-side's margin assumptions?

Request: T-I2

Peers: Swedencare; CVS Group; Zoetis; Elanco; IDEXX Laboratories; Vetoquinol; Virbac; Lifco and AddLife (for how the market discusses roll-up cash conversion and adjusted earnings) Operating conditions: falling US clinic visits; the 2026 companion-animal price war; corporate clinic consolidation and the UK CMA remedies; online prescription migration (Chewy, Covetrus VetSuite); higher-for-longer rates; a defensive rotation into healthcare in 2026 with small, leveraged names sold first Inputs:

  • Margin conversion after three quarters of double-digit organic growth (Jefferies, 28 October 2026: "Growth Proven, Margins the Next Test").
  • Net working capital rising to EUR 116.4m at Q3 2026 despite the company's statement that the Q2 receivables build would normalise.
  • Contingent-consideration revaluations in financial items, and their effect on the tax rate (35% in Q1 2026).
  • The doubling of capitalised R&D and software (EUR 7.7m in nine months of 2026 against EUR 3.6m) and its effect on EBITDA against EBITA.
  • Rising central costs (EUR 3.0m in Q3 2026 against 1.8m).
  • Related-party licensing income invoiced by Specialty Pharma to an entity owned by a segment manager (EUR 9.7m in 2025, annual report).
  • Spill-over of the dermatology price war into allergen immunotherapy.
  • US orthopaedic procedure volumes.
  • VetFamily against Covetrus VetSuite and Chewy's Modern Animal clinics.
  • Fidelio Capital's 58.4% holding and the free float.
  • The spread of broker EPS definitions (FY2026 EUR 0.089 to EUR 0.140).
  • The credibility of the 2030 target.
  • The EUR 150m bond maturing 13 May 2028. Focus: Which of these dynamics are discussed by no one or by only one broker or commentator, particularly the below-EBITA items (contingent-consideration revaluations, tax, capitalised development, EPS basis) and the related-party licensing income, and which are already widely priced?

Request: OWN-1

Question: What is licensed in the arrangement under which Vimian Group AB's Specialty Pharma segment invoiced licensing fees of EUR 9.4m (2024) and EUR 9.7m (2025) to an entity owned by a segment manager (annual report related-party note)?

  • What is the regulatory reason for routing the business through that entity, for example US state pharmacy or compounding licensing rules?
  • What cost and margin attach to the income, what are the contract term and renewal conditions, and is it at arm's length?
  • Has it continued since the Head of Specialty Pharma changed on 1 October 2026?
  • Do other US veterinary allergy-immunotherapy or compounding suppliers use similar structures?

Request: OWN-2

Question: What is the payment schedule and sensitivity of Vimian Group AB's contingent consideration?

  • It was carried at EUR 31.6m at 30 September 2026, down from EUR 46.4m at end-2025.
  • The maximum payout was EUR 142.1m across 25 obligations at Q3 2026, against EUR 113.2m across 27 in the 2025 annual report. Why did the maximum rise?
  • How are the iM3 tranches structured (a EUR 10m hurdle and an optional tranche of up to EUR 50m, Q3 2024 call)?
  • How much of the 2025-2026 discounting and probability adjustments in net financial items was non-deductible for tax?
  • How do CVS Group, Swedencare and AddLife present and forecast similar charges?

Request: OWN-3

  • Which projects, by segment (for example Clarity XR dental imaging, OvaCyte, the Pet Allergy Explorer, Nextmune Bio, systems)?
  • Over what useful lives is it amortised?
  • How does the amount compare with the capitalisation practice of Swedencare, Vetoquinol, Virbac and IDEXX?
  • How would expensing it change segment adjusted EBITA margins in 2026-2027?

Request: OWN-4

Question: What are the volumes, pricing and share of the North American veterinary orthopaedic implant market in 2024-2026, and what are the leading indicators of a recovery?

  • Market volumes and implant pricing, and the share of Vimian Group AB's Movora against Arthrex Vet Systems, the DePuy Synthes veterinary business and KYON, during Johnson & Johnson's planned separation of DePuy Synthes.
  • How far did Movora's January 2026 move from inside sales to a field sales force change sales productivity?
  • What leading indicators (referral-centre caseloads, TPLO procedure counts, financing and insurance uptake) would signal a recovery in elective orthopaedic surgery?

Request: OWN-5

Question: How did prescribing of allergen-specific immunotherapy and allergy testing behave in 2026, as symptomatic itch drugs were discounted or launched?

  • The drugs in question: Zoetis' Apoquel and Cytopoint with gross-to-net rebates; Elanco's Zenrelia at an introductory discount; Merck's Numelvi with a client guarantee.
  • What do veterinary dermatologists and general practitioners in the US and Europe say about the choice between immunotherapy and these drugs, and about their cost to owners?
  • What does this imply for the volume and pricing of Vimian Group AB's Nextmune allergy franchise and its Pet Allergy Explorer test?

Request: OWN-6

Question: What explains Vimian Group AB's rise in net working capital from EUR 96.6m at end-2025 to EUR 116.4m at 30 September 2026, with inventory of EUR 98.3m and trade receivables of EUR 67.5m (Q3 interim report)?

  • How much is MedTech inventory, consignment stock in clinics, working capital brought by acquired companies, and receivables from direct selling?
  • What happened to inventory after the discontinuation of more than 4,000 MedTech SKUs in Q4 2025?
  • How do direct-to-clinic animal-health suppliers and distributors (Swedencare, Vetoquinol, Patterson, Henry Schein) compare on working capital as a share of revenue?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 15 November 2026, 15:26 to 16:05 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Answers to the third and fourth sets of questions: Vimian Group AB (VIMIAN-SE), as at 2026-11-15

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer (context, not a question)

1. Bases. Read before using any figure. Money is in EUR millions unless stated. The second set's three earnings-per-share (EPS) bases are kept:

  • Model basis: excludes amortisation of acquired intangibles (PPA) and one-off items affecting comparability (IAC); includes about EUR 6m a year of recurring roll-up costs and all net financial items, including the non-cash ones. The Financial Analyst's model is on this basis.
  • After-PPA basis: deducts PPA after tax and adds back the non-cash contingent-consideration and currency items. This is the basis closest to most brokers and therefore to consensus.
  • Reported (IFRS).

"My arithmetic" marks figures I calculated. The first set is dated 10 November, the second 11 November and the AlphaSense reports 14 to 15 November 2026. The Capital Markets Day is on 24 November 2026, nine days after this is written.

2. The four findings of this work that change how the earlier sets should be read:

  1. The model's headline return (+45% to 31 March 2028) is flattered by a mismatch of earnings bases, not by a more optimistic forecast. The guideline fair multiple (27.07x) comes from the history of the multiple on consensus earnings, which mostly deduct PPA. The model applies it to earnings that exclude PPA. On a single basis the return is about +15% to +34% (D-01).
  2. The working-capital build is one line, not a structural feature of direct selling. Nearly all of the 2026 rise in receivables came with acquisitions. The organic build is MedTech (dental) inventory of about EUR 17m (D-01, D-03).
  3. "Dental" is a single hidden driver that runs through five lines at once: MedTech growth and margin (positive), inventory, earn-out charges and the tax rate (all negative). Success in dental raises EBITA and lowers cash flow and reported EPS in the same period (D-03).
  4. The earn-out mechanism dampens the range of reported EPS. When acquired businesses beat, the charge rises; when they miss, it reverses. Operating surprises and below-EBITA surprises therefore offset each other (D-04).

3. Changes to forecast inputs. I change two lines: net financial items and the tax rate (see inputs-changed.md and D-01). Everything else in the second set's inputs stands.

4. Reliability of the AlphaSense reports. They are useful for dated facts from company reports and broker notes. Their own conclusions are sometimes wrong or loosely worded, and I do not rely on them. Examples: one says there is "medium confidence that Vimian will meet" the 2030 target, which confuses reaffirming a target with reaching it; one describes the contingent-consideration charges as "positive financial adjustments". One figure needs checking against the Q3 report: related-party licensing income of EUR 7.7m for the first nine months of 2026 is the same number as capitalised development for the same period. Two reports give it independently, so I use it at medium confidence.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of VIMIAN-SE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-13 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Vimian Group AB (VIMIAN-SE), a Stockholm-listed companion-animal health group reporting in euros, with four segments: Specialty Pharma (Nextmune allergy diagnostics and immunotherapy, dermatology, nutrition, and Bova compounded veterinary medicines), MedTech (Movora veterinary orthopaedics, iM3 and AllAccem veterinary dentistry, Norfolk Medical Products soft-tissue surgery), Veterinary Services (VetFamily buying group for independent clinics, plus co-owned clinics) and Diagnostics (Indical livestock diagnostics, I-Vet and OvaCyte companion-animal diagnostics).

Request: OWN-1

Question: What did Vimian Group AB (VIMIAN-SE) present at its first Capital Markets Day on 24 November 2026, and how did each covering broker respond? - What was said about the target of adjusted EBITA above EUR 300m by 2030 ("over half organic", "without further capital injections", leverage below 3.0x; set March 2024, reaffirmed in the Q3 report of 22 October 2026): was it kept, re-dated, re-based, replaced or supplemented? - What new financial targets or ambitions were given, by segment and for the group: organic growth, adjusted EBITA margin, cash conversion, net working capital, return on capital, capital expenditure, tax rate, an acquisition spending envelope, leverage, dividends or buybacks? - What was said about MedTech inventory (group inventory was EUR 98.3m at 30 September 2026), about contingent consideration (EUR 31.6m carried, maximum EUR 142.1m at Q3 2026), about the related-party licensing income in Specialty Pharma, and about equity funding or larger acquisitions? - For each covering broker (UBS, Jefferies, Barclays, Nordea, DNB Carnegie and any other), what changed after the event in rating, target price and FY2026 to FY2029 estimates for revenue, adjusted EBITA or EBITDA and EPS, and what reasons did each give? Set out in particular the investment case of Barclays (Sell, target EUR 2.61 on FactSet at 22 October 2026) and of UBS (Hold, target EUR 3.19 at 4 November 2026). - How did the share price and trading volume react on the day and in the following days, and how did investor and press commentary describe the event?

Request: OWN-2

Question: On what earnings definition does each broker covering Vimian Group AB (VIMIAN-SE) publish adjusted EPS, and what valuation multiples have Vimian and its peers commanded at different stages of delivery? - For each of UBS, Jefferies, Barclays, Nordea, DNB Carnegie and any other covering broker: does its adjusted EPS deduct or add back amortisation of acquired intangibles, items affecting comparability, and the non-cash discounting and probability adjustments on contingent consideration; what tax rate does it use; and does it include unannounced acquisitions? FactSet showed FY2026 broker EPS from EUR 0.089 (UBS) to EUR 0.140 (DNB Carnegie) on 10 November 2026. - Which valuation measure does each broker use for its target price (P/E, EV/EBITA, EV/EBIT, discounted cash flow), at what multiple, and against which peer group? - What forward P/E and EV/EBITA multiples has Vimian traded on since its listing in June 2021, and what was happening to organic growth, margins, cash conversion and estimate revisions at its highest and lowest multiples? - For Swedencare, Vetoquinol, Virbac, Dechra Pharmaceuticals (before its acquisition), CVS Group, Zoetis, IDEXX Laboratories, and the Nordic serial acquirers Lifco, Addtech, AddLife and Indutrade: what multiples did each command when organic growth was strong but margins and cash conversion had not yet improved, and how did the multiple change once margins and cash conversion did, or did not, follow? Give dated examples and the size of each re-rating or de-rating.

Request: OWN-3

Question: How has the share price of Vimian Group AB (VIMIAN-SE) reacted to its own news and to its owners' actions, and what does that show about the size and suddenness of price risk? - What was the share price move on the day of each quarterly report from Q1 2024 to Q3 2026, against what consensus surprise in organic growth, adjusted EBITA, EPS and operating cash flow, and which line did commentary say drove the move? - What were the price moves and stated causes around the removal of the chief executive (17 July 2025), the April 2024 rights issue, the 2023 patent settlement of USD 70m, and the fall to about SEK 28 in February 2026? - Has Fidelio Capital (58.4% of capital), any board member, any member of management or any former segment head bought or sold shares since January 2025; are there lock-up undertakings; has Fidelio stated its intentions for its holding or the life of the fund that holds it; and how have the holdings of Handelsbanken Fonder, Capital Group and other large institutions changed? - What short positions have been disclosed, what is the free float, and how does daily trading volume compare with the size of any past placings? - How did Vimian's shares react on the days of large moves in Zoetis, IDEXX Laboratories, Elanco and Swedencare in 2025 and 2026? - For comparable sponsor-controlled or thinly traded Nordic acquirers and animal-health companies, what happened to the share price when a controlling holder placed shares or when an equity-funded acquisition was announced?
